# PilotODIN 1.0 | Current continuation checkpoint

## 14 November 2026 | N12 Cache Manager operations before recorder stop

- [Hosted Windows run 43612214016, job 132499495498](https://github.com/Sonkhe-Shongwe/PilotODIN/actions/runs/43612214016/job/132499495498) passed attempt 1 at `51ed5fc6ec481336260cb1efc63e56377fd428ea`. I added a 20-second wait after calibration and before requesting WPR stop, with the generated comparison handles still open. The measured wait was 20,000.416 ms. The companion export validated 66,597 events, the rundown recovered 632/632 names, both known application stack controls passed, and the decoders reported no loss or decoding errors. The 139 local N12 regressions passed.
- **WPR stop is unnecessary for this observed execution path.** The protected System paging write and EOF resize were requested 20,494.422 ms and 20,494.367 ms before WPR stop, respectively. Both completed before stop. Their requests followed the return of the deliberate fixture write on the same file by 286.227 ms and 286.282 ms. The unflushed outside file exhibited the corresponding pair before stop. All four matched the previous Cache Manager lazy-write execution paths, with 16/20 resolved write frames and 14/19 resolved resize frames on each file. The two operations preceded the start of the added wait and were observed because the recorder stayed running.
- The separate flushed and untouched comparisons each had verified identities and zero matching successful System writes or resizes. The strict reducer counted exactly two deliberate fixture writes and retained the protected System write and resize violations.
- **N12 PARTIAL / strict coverage BLOCKED / P4 ACTIVE at 65/100.** The observed timing rules out WPR stop as a prerequisite for these events. A causal link to a specific earlier operation remains unverified. A follow-up can give the unflushed test files deliberately staggered writes with distinct payload ranges and check which later paging operations follow each write while monitoring the same file identities. Historical R06 attribution and physical R03 offline acceptance remain open.

## 14 November 2026 | N12 protected Cache Manager execution path

- [Hosted Windows run 43611212463, job 132496445287](https://github.com/Sonkhe-Shongwe/PilotODIN/actions/runs/43611212463/job/132496445287) passed attempt 1 at `0001b0cbc24e2bbfd48e8eb7b8949e1c1b2c3cb6`. The strict reducer counted exactly two fixture writes, verified the protected and outside controls and retained both System-context protected violations. Both known application stack controls passed. The companion export validated 59,081 events, the rundown decoded 308/308 names and the trace included 21,743 stacks with zero reported loss or decode errors.
- **Immediate execution path verified.** The protected paging write resolved 16 of 20 frames, including `CcAsyncLazywriteWorkerThread`, `CcWriteBehindAsync`, `CcFlushCacheOneRange`, `MmFlushSection` and `MiIssueAsynchronousFlush`. The protected EOF resize resolved 14 of 19 frames, including the same lazy-write worker, `CcWriteBehindPostProcess` and `CcSetValidData`. The unflushed outside file had the same resolved frames for each operation. Both pairs completed during WPR stop. The verified flushed and untouched files had zero matching successful System writes or resizes.
- The first [symbol lookup](https://github.com/Sonkhe-Shongwe/PilotODIN/actions/runs/43610711208/job/132494933526) and [direct PDB attempt](https://github.com/Sonkhe-Shongwe/PilotODIN/actions/runs/43611027413/job/132495878745) passed fixture and calibration checks but produced no function names and failed the symbol gate. The passing resolver matched trace image metadata to the runner binaries, checked public PDB GUID and age against PE CodeView, and used exact public symbol addresses or x64 unwind ranges. Raw stack addresses and captures stayed in runner temp. Local checks passed 139 N12 regressions plus focused image, PE, PDB and redaction checks.
- **N12 PARTIAL / strict coverage BLOCKED / P4 ACTIVE at 65/100.** The Windows Cache Manager path is verified for these synthetic events. The earlier application action and any causal role of WPR stop remain unverified. A next attribution test needs independent evidence linking a specific initiating operation to these later requests. Historical R06 attribution and physical R03 offline acceptance remain open.

The missing calibration write has no proven cause in the redacted logs.

## 14 November 2026 | Hosted offline analytics model comparison

- [Run #43593329731, job #132442500969](https://github.com/Sonkhe-Shongwe/PilotODIN/actions/runs/43593329731/job/132442500969) completed successfully on attempt 1 at `6301ceccf073045d510123c007f5622c23c935aa`. Models ran sequentially on pinned llama.cpp b10344 with CPU inference, 2,048-token context, and identical structured prompts capped at 128 tokens. The single hosted approval is consumed.
- Qwen3 1.7B: **2,055.8 MiB** peak resident, **1,089.9 MiB** peak commit, 2.500 s to readiness, 1.766–5.469 s per request. **2/4** passed. It wrapped the correct overview fields in an extra object and supplied a log-status string for the unknown cause.
- Qwen3 0.6B: **934.9 MiB** peak resident, **619.0 MiB** peak commit, 1.781 s to readiness, 0.922–1.891 s per request. **2/4** passed. It omitted the known missing-row count and the known false training status by returning null.
- SmolLM2 360M: **420.0 MiB** peak resident, **218.6 MiB** peak commit, 0.531 s to readiness, 1.000–1.985 s per request. **1/4** passed. It used a denominator of 100 where 60 was required, supplied a cause from a field name, and returned the training result in the wrong structure.
- SmolLM2's hosted peak is 125 MiB below the owner's earlier 545 MiB available with the desktop open. This cross-host comparison gives no physical safety margin or paging evidence. No candidate cleared the quality screen. Keep verified counts, denominators, unknowns and training status under the deterministic engine's control. A future evaluation can test whether a small model adds useful wording when those facts are fixed. The 2.00 GiB admission guard and pending physical Qwen approval are unaffected. P4 stays active at 65/100.

## 14 November 2026 | Native rundown now traverses the strict reducer for pre-opened controls

- **Implementation commits:** [`de01e04`](https://github.com/Sonkhe-Shongwe/PilotODIN/commit/de01e04a20509e2f55ec1b0e5eb57386a62c43af) connects the hosted control fixture to strict ETW reduction; [`c4988d4`](https://github.com/Sonkhe-Shongwe/PilotODIN/commit/c4988d4a512c1a68e1b17ae5df4f411b73aacd8d) adds redacted lifetime-guard diagnostics; [`ea606ba`](https://github.com/Sonkhe-Shongwe/PilotODIN/commit/ea606ba862254c71395df2a6286ba5017d001fae) treats Event 10 NameCreate as corroborating evidence only when its unique canonical name matches the rundown; [`341b188`](https://github.com/Sonkhe-Shongwe/PilotODIN/commit/341b18883b922521f808289140a895602f574fa2) corrects the ambiguous-name regression assertion; [`57d6115`](https://github.com/Sonkhe-Shongwe/PilotODIN/commit/57d61151917b2391941c27693a7312b1e7548ea9) separates control-specific checks from unrelated global violations.
- **Strict reducer integration:** The new `check-etw-preopened-rundown` CLI feeds the actual native rundown JSONL and the same ETW event export through `inspect_etw_files`. It captures the exact control FileKeys in memory and verifies that the strict reducer itself recovered both keys; it does not rely solely on the independent `n12_etw_correlate.py` path report. The collector PID has a fixture-scoped known lifetime because it predates the ETW session; this generated test identity is not application process attribution.
- **Name evidence rule:** Event 10 NameCreate is no longer treated as an unconditional lifetime break. It corroborates the rundown only when the observed canonical name is unique and exactly matches the digest-pinned rundown. Conflicting or ambiguous NameCreate paths remain unresolved. Event 11 NameDelete and the existing create/open, close, rename, delete, link and unsupported transitions continue to invalidate the mapping.
- **Exact-source dedicated N12 Windows run:** [Run #43555662518](https://github.com/Sonkhe-Shongwe/PilotODIN/actions/runs/43555662518) passed 50/50 focused tests and decoded 573/573 native rundown records. TDH metadata, FileObject and FileName succeeded for all 573; decoder conflicts, undecoded records and callback errors were zero; ProcessTrace and CloseTrace returned status 0. The strict reducer recovered both control keys, detected the protected write, kept the outside write outside protected roots and left zero unresolved control mutations. The redacted report still contains unrelated protected-root resize/write violations and unrelated unresolved mutations; global strict coverage remains **BLOCKED**.
- **Exact-source broader P4 regression:** [Run #43555662517](https://github.com/Sonkhe-Shongwe/PilotODIN/actions/runs/43555662517) passed all three jobs: 235 Python tests, 78 Node checks, TypeScript/Vite production build and 13 Chromium scenarios. Its hosted Windows job exported 37,702 ETW events, decoded 617/617 native rundown records with complete TDH/FileObject/FileName success, and repeated the strict pre-opened control integration. Unrelated protected-root violations and unresolved `other` mutations remained in the redacted output.
- **Retained failure chronology:** [Runs #43554609677](https://github.com/Sonkhe-Shongwe/PilotODIN/actions/runs/43554609677) and [#43554857646](https://github.com/Sonkhe-Shongwe/PilotODIN/actions/runs/43554857646) exposed Event 10 over-invalidation; [#43555323995](https://github.com/Sonkhe-Shongwe/PilotODIN/actions/runs/43555323995) caught an incorrect negative-test expectation for ambiguous names; [#43555412832](https://github.com/Sonkhe-Shongwe/PilotODIN/actions/runs/43555412832) exposed unrelated protected-root violations being incorrectly treated as control failures. All failed runs remain intact and were not reclassified.
- **Acceptance boundary:** N12 remains partial and P4 remains active. The controls prove strict path recovery and classification for the generated pre-opened handles, not full PilotODIN startup-to-exit mutation attribution or complete background-writer attribution. ## 14 November 2026 | Rundown and ETW temporal alignment enforced

- **Safety finding:** A valid rundown digest alone does not prove that the stop-time snapshot belongs to the event-observation window. Without a time bound, a rundown captured after ETW stopped could supply a stale path for an earlier write because later rename, close or reuse events would be invisible to the reducer.
- **Collector correction:** The pre-opened-handle collector now stops WPR while the companion logman ETW session is still recording, records the WPR stop-completion upper bound as `rundown_stop_ticks`, and stops logman afterward. The Windows manifest records both `etw.rundown_stop_utc` and `etw.rundown_sha256`. The PowerShell fixture asserts the rundown stop lies inside the companion ETW start/end window.
- **Reducer correction:** `read_rundown_file` requires all three timestamps and rejects the rundown unless `etw.start_utc <= etw.rundown_stop_utc <= etw.end_utc`. The ETW event export therefore observes changes after the rundown snapshot through ETW stop. Normal captures without rundown remain supported; the PowerShell manifest builder checks for the optional property before reading it.
- **Retained failure:** [P4 run #43542890535](https://github.com/Sonkhe-Shongwe/PilotODIN/actions/runs/43542890535) failed because a normal capture correctly lacked `rundown_stop_ticks`, but the first PowerShell implementation assumed the property existed. [Commit `6f6119ea`](https://github.com/Sonkhe-Shongwe/PilotODIN/commit/6f6119ea2e85cba7498dbfb33d0dbaabba66be65) made the field optional for normal captures. The failed run remains in history.
- **Exact-source N12 verification:** [Run #43543476762](https://github.com/Sonkhe-Shongwe/PilotODIN/actions/runs/43543476762) passed 49/49 focused tests. The native collector printed that the rundown stop timestamp was inside the companion ETW window, then decoded 382/382 rundown records. TDH metadata, FileObject and FileName properties succeeded for every record; decoder conflicts, undecoded records and callback errors were zero. ProcessTrace and CloseTrace succeeded. Both controlled pre-opened targets matched their exact paths, successful completions and issuing-thread identities.
- **Exact-source P4 regression:** [Run #43543476744](https://github.com/Sonkhe-Shongwe/PilotODIN/actions/runs/43543476744) passed all three jobs: 234 Python tests, 78 Node checks, TypeScript/Vite production build, 13 Chromium scenarios, synthetic Windows preservation/hash checks and the N12 native fixture. The Windows job also verified the rundown timestamp inside the ETW window and decoded 337/337 native rundown records with both pre-opened control paths matching.
- **CI trigger maintenance:** [Commit `6f295c82`](https://github.com/Sonkhe-Shongwe/PilotODIN/commit/6f295c827f43b4259207a174f97542354fad28c5) added the native ETW probe to the focused N12 workflow path filter so future capture-orchestration changes trigger the rundown checks.
- **Acceptance boundary:** N12 remains partial. These generated hosted controls do not prove that the actual PilotODIN application has complete startup-to-exit file-mutation attribution, that every background writer is accounted for, or that the live combined Sysmon/ETW calibration passes. R03 physical 8 GB/offline evidence, R06 disposition and owner acceptance remain open. The one-time combined live workflow remains disabled.
- **Next at this checkpoint (completed in the following section).** Feed the actual native rundown JSONL from the hosted Windows fixture into strict reducer correlation for the two pre-opened controls. Verify that the protected write is detected, the outside write is not classified as protected, and neither control has an unresolved path, while unrelated unresolved mutations continue to block. Do not treat this generated fixture as live application acceptance.

## 13 November 2026 | R04 Windows drag-and-drop correction

- The hosted R04 failure occurred with Tauri's native drop handler enabled by default. The workspace tabs rely on HTML drag events. [Tauri v2's configuration reference](https://v2.tauri.app/reference/config/#dragdropenabled) says its native handler must be disabled for HTML drag and drop on Windows. This is a confirmed configuration mismatch. The historical run did not record which drag events WebView2 received.
- [`7a1bae095c3445074924c5e876cc6df14bf8a50b`](https://github.com/Sonkhe-Shongwe/PilotODIN/commit/7a1bae095c3445074924c5e876cc6df14bf8a50b) sets `dragDropEnabled: false` for the main production and isolated development windows. The shell prevents external files and links from navigating away when dropped onto the workspace. File inputs and internal tab drag types keep their handling. The existing Chromium docking test checks both external drop types and the actual tab drag with zero backend writes.
- [P4 #43416444376](https://github.com/Sonkhe-Shongwe/PilotODIN/actions/runs/43416444376) passed at `7a1bae095c3445074924c5e876cc6df14bf8a50b` (183 Python, 77 Node, TypeScript/Vite, 12 Chromium and synthetic Windows checks). [P1 #43416444662](https://github.com/Sonkhe-Shongwe/PilotODIN/actions/runs/43416444662) failed because an import-test source assertion scanned the later docking test. [`01038512460dee0ec469e0f51781d734e1f75d98`](https://github.com/Sonkhe-Shongwe/PilotODIN/commit/01038512460dee0ec469e0f51781d734e1f75d98) bounds the assertion to its own scenario. [P1 #43416564433](https://github.com/Sonkhe-Shongwe/PilotODIN/actions/runs/43416564433) passed at that test-only follow-up (187 Node, TypeScript/Vite and 7 Chromium scenarios).

## 12 November 2026 | P4-54 independent real-data fixed-origin calendar baseline: NO-FIT audit

- **Meaningful negative historical result:** public user-uploaded daily UCI Bike Sharing data (731 rows) under the predeclared P4-52 first-584 vs last-147 chronological periods. A reference rule using development-only **month × workingday mean** has 147-day holdout **MAE 2,353.14**, **worse** than the development-only constant median **2,259.16** (by 93.98/day, ~4.16%). Month-only 2,387.14, month×weekday 2,375.19, weekday-only 2,287.17; these diagnostics do **not** justify holdout-based model selection. Computed independently in JS on the checked-in fixture and Python/pandas on the uploaded CSV, no ML fit/experiment API.
- **Critical comparability finding:** 24 development month×workingday categories, minimum group count 9, final holdout unknown groups **0**; but expanding CV folds had respectively **141/146, 61/146, 0/146** dates with unseen month×workingday groups, forcing fallback means. Mean daily rentals rose from **4,153.60** development to **5,897.80** holdout; Sep-Dec 2011 **3,609.99** vs 2012 **5,686.81**. These are period differences, not proven causes or model predictions.
- **Decision boundary:** current rolling previous-day/7-day/week-ago references are conditional on report latency, and **not equivalent** to a 147-day fixed-origin forecast. P4-54 documents frozen horizon, timestamp/as-of availability, cold-category/2013 unknown-year risk, holdout reuse concern and business-owner dependency before any separately approved research fit. See [P4-54 report](./P4_54_PUBLIC_BIKE_FIXED_ORIGIN_CALENDAR_BASELINE_2026-11-12.md). **No new source-code change, CI, native build, model fitting or owner original-data access.**
- **Gate status unchanged:** Real business Gate C **UNVERIFIED**, historical original whole app-data-tree R06 **STRICT FAIL** (320 backup vs 346 files, 74 browser/cache candidates and unknown past writer), **P4 ACTIVE / R01–R06 OPEN, P5 NOT AUTHORISED, P8 NOT CERTIFIED**.

## 12 November 2026 | P4-43 read-only original preservation stability, exact-source Windows CI PASS

- **P4-R06 source-confirmed false-confidence condition:** `qa/p4_native/Compare_P4_Original_Storage.ps1` previously scanned the independently verified pre-QA backup and current original app-data tree only once, then calculated file and folder change counts and EXE integrity. A source mutation after the initial current/backup scan but before the output could leave stale counts and even a **FULL_FINGERPRINT_MATCH** verdict. This was a code-level comparison vulnerability, **not evidence of a new owner-data write**.
- **Bounded read-only fix:** [`0ff02440`](https://github.com/Sonkhe-Shongwe/PilotODIN/commit/0ff0244093b5be05f7bcb0eb746d37811303c650) adds a second complete read-only scan of **both backup and original-data tree**, plus a second original EXE SHA256. The comparator rejects with an exception and **does not emit a falsely stable result** when file paths, content hashes, sizes, empty-directory membership or executable digest change between the two sequential observations. The new `sequential_rescan_consistent=true` field is emitted only for stable comparisons alongside `snapshot_is_atomic=false`. The historical P4-22 v1 file-only baseline remains untouched. Two sequential reads are **not** an atomic filesystem snapshot and cannot establish past writer identity or guarantee no future write.
- **Deterministic disposable Windows regression:** The test fixture deliberately mutates either a **current original-data synthetic file** or an **independent backup synthetic file** when the initial original EXE hash is collected after the first tree scan. Such post-scan changes previously could return a false full match; they now **fail closed**, and restoring clean disposable files yields a limited full match. Synthetic tests remain restricted to ephemeral Windows CI runner files. Three earlier [#43148792745](https://github.com/Sonkhe-Shongwe/PilotODIN/actions/runs/43148792745), [#43148981103](https://github.com/Sonkhe-Shongwe/PilotODIN/actions/runs/43148981103) and [#43149155782](https://github.com/Sonkhe-Shongwe/PilotODIN/actions/runs/43149155782) **FAILED** due to test-harness hash interception scope/PowerShell 5.1 compatibility. Corrections [`78bc25c7`](https://github.com/Sonkhe-Shongwe/PilotODIN/commit/78bc25c7e73f37b39f9b7cf3fe043cf532f075fb), [`9304f217`](https://github.com/Sonkhe-Shongwe/PilotODIN/commit/9304f2170f770247a3551485ee6fec63c59dd63c) and [`e6803505`](https://github.com/Sonkhe-Shongwe/PilotODIN/commit/e680350500e1634b0599dc3926068439e37bb48b) isolated the interception to a disposable child scope. The failed runs are preserved as failures and **not** retroactively counted PASS.
- **Final exact-source verification:** [P4 GitHub Actions #43149480761](https://github.com/Sonkhe-Shongwe/PilotODIN/actions/runs/43149480761) **COMPLETED SUCCESS 3/3** at source `e680350500e1634b0599dc3926068439e37bb48b`. ## 11 November 2026 | P4-40 categorical datetime outcome guard; exact-source CI PASS

- **Source-confirmed synthetic gap:** A pandas `CategoricalDtype` holding datetime64 or timezone-aware timestamps returns false to `is_datetime64_any_dtype(series)`. Therefore an outcome-named column with only two timestamp categories could evade P4's date/time-target rejection and be presented as a reviewable classification hypothesis. It is a dtype-specific **source/synthetic** finding, not owner business evidence.
- **Narrow source fix:** [`0387412e`](https://github.com/Sonkhe-Shongwe/PilotODIN/commit/0387412e8d0789144edcffe49c62b5ac496bc2ac) changes only `backend/target_opportunities.py` and `tests/test_p4_target_opportunities.py`. The existing `_is_date` predicate checks the underlying value dtype using the previously implemented read-only `_value_dtype` helper, instead of the categorical wrapper dtype. Two datetime categorical regression variants (naive/timezone-aware) now abstain in both queue and manual P4 review; ordinary categorical classification and numeric time-to-resolution regression remain structurally reviewable, never training-authorised. No DataFrame edits, imputation, model fitting, dataset access, changed APIs, dependencies, workflows or installer builds.
- **Exact-source CI:** [P4 Actions #43089658871](https://github.com/Sonkhe-Shongwe/PilotODIN/actions/runs/43089658871) at `0387412e` **COMPLETED SUCCESS, attempt 1, three jobs**. Logs independently show **147 Python tests PASS, 40 Node tests PASS/0 fail, TS/Vite build PASS, 9 disposable real-HTTP Chromium tests PASS**, plus Windows-2022 synthetic data-integrity/WebView and PowerShell 7/5.1 checks PASS. This later documentation-only commit has **no separate application CI**. Latest native WebView2 acceptance NOT RETESTED.
- **Unchanged hard NO-GO:** Previous independent original production EXE/backup hashes matched and protected project `data/` subtree had zero detected differences, but **strict complete original app-data tree preservation FAIL** persists (320 backup vs 346 observed, 74 WebView/cache *candidates*, historical writer UNKNOWN). Real owner 8 GB offline Qwen, source-matched owner-native UI and independent real-business Gate C remain NOT TESTED / UNVERIFIED. P4-R01–R06 OPEN; owner refused risk deferral. **P4 ACTIVE; P5 NOT AUTHORISED; P8 NOT CERTIFIED**. Owner's no-intermediate-installers order through P4–P7 remains binding; P8 final packaging requires separate explicit authorisation.

## 11 November 2026 | P4-39 numeric categorical non-finite screening bypass fixed; exact-source CI PASS

- **Current-source/synthetic finding:** `backend/target_opportunities.py::_candidate` previously used `pd.api.types.is_numeric_dtype(series)` directly for target infinity/complex checks and predictor finite-value checks. This predicate is false for a pandas numeric **CategoricalDtype** Series, so `pd.Categorical([+inf,-inf]*40)` could bypass numeric-label validation, or numeric categorical predictors could falsely appear to vary through infinities. This was reproduced with pandas dtype probes and source inspection; no owner-data incident is claimed.
- **Bounded fix:** [implementation `8a774521`](https://github.com/Sonkhe-Shongwe/PilotODIN/commit/8a77452128126f8a0f333d641ff5beb6f449f17d) changes exactly `backend/target_opportunities.py` and `tests/test_p4_target_opportunities.py`. A read-only `_value_dtype` helper inspects the observed categorical values' dtype for existing numeric/complex and finite-predictor policy. No file writes, input DataFrame/category mutation, data cleaning, automatic target selection, ML training or packaging. Tests cover three invalid categorical targets, three invalid categorical predictors, finite numeric category control, and complex-valued category rejection, with manual/queue review and original-data immutability assertions.
- **Exact-source CI verified:** [P4 workflow #43088605156](https://github.com/Sonkhe-Shongwe/PilotODIN/actions/runs/43088605156), **COMPLETED SUCCESS, attempt 1, 3/3 jobs at `8a774521`**. Decoded logs: **144 Python pytest PASS; 40 Node tests PASS (0 failed); TS/Vite build PASS; 9/9 disposable real-HTTP Chromium tests PASS; synthetic Windows-2022 original-storage and WebView/PowerShell 7 and 5.1 checks PASS**. This later documentation-only commit is **not** assigned new application CI.
- **Safety / phase:** No intermediate installer was produced, downloaded or installed, and no owner data/project was touched. Owner's P4–P7 installer freeze remains binding. Earlier independent original production EXE+backup integrity matches and zero protected original `data/` changes remain historical evidence, but **strict full original app-data tree preservation FAIL** persists (320 baseline vs 346 current; 74 browser/cache *candidates*, historic writer UNKNOWN). Latest-source physical Windows UI, owner offline Qwen/8 GB and independent real-business Gate C remain NOT RETESTED / UNVERIFIED. **P4-R01–R06 OPEN; P4 ACTIVE; P5 NOT AUTHORISED; P8 NOT CERTIFIED.** [Focused P4-39 report](./P4_39_NUMERIC_CATEGORICAL_NONFINITE_SCREENING_2026-11-11.md).
- **Next authorised action:** Audit only verified, materially relevant remaining P4 negative/version/data-integrity gaps; avoid cycling through already tested scenarios. Handle mandatory owner-native/offline and independent business Gate C only with genuine owner evidence and owner-approved release-phase constraints. Do not weaken acceptance conditions or start P5.

## 11 November 2026 | P4-37 non-finite numeric predictor structural false-positive corrected; CI PASS

- **Source-confirmed P4 screening defect:** `backend/target_opportunities.py::_candidate` already rejected non-finite **numeric target labels**, but the predictor variation check counted infinities as distinct nonmissing feature values. Synthetic 80-row target `churn=["Y","N"]*40` plus `signal=[+inf,-inf]*40` could therefore be labelled technically reviewable despite **no finite predictor variation**. A column alternating one valid number and infinity had the same false-support problem. This is a source/synthetic correctness finding, **not evidence from actual owner data**.
- **Bounded source fix:** [`3b36626b`](https://github.com/Sonkhe-Shongwe/PilotODIN/commit/3b36626b30f302d02e1edc965478fa72a5eecca4) updates only `backend/target_opportunities.py` and `tests/test_p4_target_opportunities.py`: within the existing limited labelled-row predictor screening, mask nonfinite numeric values **in a local view**, then require more than one distinct *finite* value for numeric predictor variation. Repeated blank-only/padded outcome safeguards remain. No mutation, automatic cleanup, model training, endpoint modification, owner-data access, or native installation. Both queue and manual structural review use the shared check.
- **Exact-source verified CI:** [P4 workflow #43085745069](https://github.com/Sonkhe-Shongwe/PilotODIN/actions/runs/43085745069) **COMPLETED SUCCESS at `3b36626b`, all three jobs**: new synthetic infinity-only, one-finite-plus-infinity, nan/infinity and two-finite control cases; existing Python P4 and Node source contracts, TS/Vite build; disposable real-HTTP Chromium; fake-file Windows integrity/PowerShell 5.1. The later docs-only continuity commit has **no separate application CI**. Real owner native UI remains **NOT RETESTED**.
- **Scope limit:** Presence of a numeric predictor containing infinity is **not generally certified safe** just because another feature contains two finite observed values; this increment only prevents invalid-only/constant-finite numeric predictors from satisfying structural variation. The later actual data-cleaning, final predictor choice and training gates remain separate.
- **Owner installer freeze:** No installer, NSIS artifact, owner-PC install, original dataset edit or new release. All intermediate installer/verification jobs remain disabled on this branch until final P8 with fresh explicit owner approval.
- **Unchanged binding NO-GO:** independent original production EXE and backup matched baseline, protected original `data/` store showed 0 differences, but **strict original full data-directory preservation FAIL** remains (320 baseline vs 346 current, 74 WebView/cache *candidates*, historical writer UNKNOWN). Actual owner offline Qwen/8 GB, latest source native UI parity and independent real business Gate C remain open; P4-R01–R06 OPEN. **P4 ACTIVE; P5 NOT AUTHORISED; P8 NOT CERTIFIED.** Details: [P4-37](./P4_37_NONFINITE_PREDICTOR_STRUCTURAL_GUARD_2026-11-11.md).

## 9 November 2026 | P4-20 labelled-row predictor screening defect source-fixed, exact CI 108/28/8 PASS

- **P4 risk-first instruction:** User said `next`; no approval to accept residual risk/close P4 or start P5. [P4-20 scope and evidence ledger](./P4_20_LABELLED_ROW_PREDICTOR_OVERLAP_SAFETY_AUDIT_2026-11-09.md).
- **Source-confirmed structural false readiness:** `_candidate` previously treated an input predictor as usable if it varied anywhere in the dataset, even when all varying observations occurred only in rows with **missing target labels**. On an 80-row synthetic `churn` frame with 40 observed labels and 40 missing, the sole `feature` could be missing or constant throughout *all labelled rows* and still count as a predictor. [Fix `1d8e4b8`](https://github.com/Sonkhe-Shongwe/PilotODIN/commit/1d8e4b8d18e21c3d2508f42f53632c2f09644e2f) restricts structural predictor variability screening to `frame.loc[series.notna()]`, shared by queue and manual target inspection. Four new synthetic cases cover both false-positive patterns, manual fail-closed path and a valid partially labelled control.

P4-14 now caps its **queue screening** at **5,000 leading rows, first 100 headers, at most 64 predictor headers per candidate and up to 64 named candidate headers**. Normal smaller datasets retain deterministic rank and exact existing shortlist (top 3 + up to 12 alternatives + 5 blocked examples).

At most five named columns within the first header window get **sample-only blocked evidence**, never approved training targets. `queue_resource_review` discloses examined/original row and header counts, inspected candidates, resource ceilings and reasons for incompleteness. For datasets larger than 30k rows, a local ML row-cap **caution** is preserved without asserting a counted full labelled population. ## 8 November 2026 | Root-cause repair: W5 fetch sandbox was swallowing Tauri IPC
- **Owner's reproduced native evidence:** Windows WebView2 W5 displayed the reviewed Region 2-cell v1 correction and empty current-version descriptions, but auto/manual Finish returned `Unclassified synthetic QA finish failure.` despite successful Rust/Tauri build. The Cargo bin/lib filename collision is an independent build warning, not the exit error.
- **Concrete source conflict:** `p3NativeQualityW5Qa.tsx` overrides **all** `window.fetch` to block unapproved network writes. Tauri's fetch-first IPC implementation invokes Rust through `ipc://localhost/<command>` (Windows WebView2 translates to `http://ipc.localhost/<command>`). The QA fetch wrapper incorrectly returned a *resolved synthetic HTTP 403 Response* for the native `finish_p3_w5_synthetic_qa` command. That breaks Tauri's native command transport and can produce exactly the observed opaque Response rejection, with no Rust rejection diagnostic. This is a source-grounded mechanism; the owner's subsequent physical retry is still needed to verify resolution.
- **Minimal scoped fix:** `frontend/src/p3NativeW5IpcGuard.ts` permits only the exact `POST /finish_p3_w5_synthetic_qa` request on Tauri's two local IPC authorities, with Tauri-Invoke-Key, Tauri-Callback and Tauri-Error headers; no query, other port or unrelated path. In the QA fetch wrapper, such a request goes to the *captured original native fetch*. All other IPC and external writes stay blocked. Existing Rust debug/exact-W5-URL/main-window gate and frontend explicit single consent, server v1, empty semantics checks remain mandatory. Production application fetch, close rules, project data and W1-W4 are untouched. An executable Node test covers accepted and rejected IPC routes; error display now distinguishes unusual/HTTP IPC failures instead of saying "unclassified".
- **Important:** Does not update a currently running Windows process, does not grant Quality approval, does not itself prove native acceptance or close P3. Another attempt may only be justified after source CI passes, previous synthetic process is safely ended, branch synced and the exact W5 window relaunched. The owner's future status-only ZIP still needs independent review. P3 ACTIVE; P4 unauthorised; P8 uncertified.

## 8 November 2026 | W5 exit request now acknowledges IPC before destroying synthetic WebView
- **Observed:** User's physical Windows W5 reaches v1 after the exact approved 2-row Region change and retains empty v1 descriptions, but clicking Finish shows a generic/unclassified failure while the native executable remains running. The terminal's Cargo bin/lib output-filename collision is a **warning**, followed by a successful debug build and process startup, not an explanation for the UI exit failure.

## Latest P2 engineering checkpoint | 5 November 2026 | Mounted incoming evidence withdrawal and stale consent
- **Confirmed source risk and production fix:** An incoming `preselectEvidenceId` could be withdrawn or replaced after the real Deliver Keep/Discard confirmation appeared, yet `pendingDiscard` still held the obsolete ID and the click handler checked only `projectId`. ## Current P2 engineering checkpoint | 5 November 2026 | Explore-to-Deliver handoff and cross-workspace draft consent
- **Material P2 data-loss prevention:** Deliver's `preselectEvidenceId` handler previously cleared authored title, narrative and evidence before checking for unsaved changes. It now validates exact eligible project evidence and in-flight write state, then demands the existing in-app **Keep editing / Discard and continue** choice before replacing a dirty mounted report. A cancelled handoff is consumed and cannot silently reappear during evidence refresh; the exact old view/draft guard is invalidated only upon approved application. An incoming invalid ID leaves user work intact. No source evidence, saved report, project SQLite or Quality changes.
- **Previously missed primary view exit:** Deliver was not included in the existing shell's memory-only edited-primary marker, so switching from an edited Deliver to Explore or another primary stage could unmount the report without route consent. `ReportWorkspace` now reports only dirty/clean status to the `App`, and `UnifiedShell` guards leaving Deliver for **any** other primary view, along with native close/browser reload/project switch. The original Prepare/Model/Predict Canvas-only guard stays unchanged. Explicitly approved route preapproval uses the actual destination, including tab close. An Explore saved snapshot ID is only queued in `App` AFTER route approval.

- **Added:** `backend/project_lifecycle.py`, additive `project_lifecycle` SQLite metadata table, active-only project selector/get protection, `GET /api/projects/manage`, `PUT /api/projects/{id}/lifecycle` with exact `rename|archive|restore`, mandatory expected revision, transactional stale-change 409, duplicate/invalid-name refusal, active training/ODIN archive refusal. No new permanent DELETE endpoint. Existing project source, Quality versions, experiments, investigations, models and reports are retained on archive.
- **UI:** `Manage projects` header action, keyboard-dialog with focus return, two active/archived lists, rename, guarded archive of **other than current** project, and restore. On rename, the active Studio project display changes without intentionally remounting or dropping unsaved Prepare fields. Native UX is unverified.
- **Verified automated, all green on exact SHA:** [P1 lifecycle backend run #41827678997](https://github.com/Sonkhe-Shongwe/PilotODIN/actions/runs/41827678997) on source ancestor `3fde4449`, **9/9 pytest PASS** (lifecycle, existing import rollback and failed-switch recovery), production backend code unchanged afterward; [P1 frontend run #41830669671](https://github.com/Sonkhe-Shongwe/PilotODIN/actions/runs/41830669671), **143/143 Node, TS/Vite build and 5/5 isolated Chromium PASS**; [P1 real-HTTP run #41830669685](https://github.com/Sonkhe-Shongwe/PilotODIN/actions/runs/41830669685), **all three real Uvicorn/Chromium scenarios 1/1 PASS**, including new synthetic D/E project manager rename→archive→restore→real backend restart, project identity and Quality v0 audit preserved.
- **Intermediate failures not concealed:** initial lifecycle workflow schema `#41827544482` failed before jobs; first UI wiring/typechecks/contract and keyboard-order attempts failed, then corrected; new D/E real-HTTP `#41829742853` / `#41830288152` refused a permitted rename because its **test-only** request guard incorrectly included a project URL ID in the JSON body match. Fixed by keeping exact project ID constrained in URL and exact operation/revision/name constrained in JSON, with final green run. CI test-only failures are not represented as production defects or PASS.
- **Open:** permanent delete remains **NOT IMPLEMENTED** pending scoped retention/rollback/recovery design and dedicated user consent. Archive/restore across multiple native WebView2 windows, existing active projects on owner Windows and other full acceptance gates are **NOT TESTED**. No local user project, file, SQLite DB, installer, AI model, release or Cargo resources was accessed or changed by this increment.
- **Next:** preserve one-primary-phase P1 focus. Complete outstanding native acceptance and closure on disposable Windows DEV data only with owner participation. Do not silently treat the new feature as desktop accepted or P1 CLOSED. ## Latest verified increment: read-only project deletion impact (4 November 2026)
**Tested SHA:** [`ebd10e2db992`](https://github.com/Sonkhe-Shongwe/PilotODIN/commit/ebd10e2db992ee6bc4ec8dc53a436d156d226458). Additive `backend/project_delete_impact.py` and revision-pinned `GET /api/projects/{id}/deletion-impact`, plus `Review impact` in the project manager for active/archived entries. Counts known direct and indirect SQLite records and identifies managed source/model directory candidates without reading dataset bytes or changing SQLite/files; unknown tables, external source paths, symlinked/unknown model locations and active training/ODIN runs are flagged. API returns `preview_only: true` and `deletion_available: false`; **no DELETE endpoint or purge control**.

**Verified hosted:** [12/12 backend pytest](https://github.com/Sonkhe-Shongwe/PilotODIN/actions/runs/41832930061) at unchanged backend ancestor `02d6da06`, [144/144 frontend Node + TS/Vite build + 5/5 Chromium](https://github.com/Sonkhe-Shongwe/PilotODIN/actions/runs/41833432539) and [3/3 actual disposable real-Uvicorn/Chromium cases](https://github.com/Sonkhe-Shongwe/PilotODIN/actions/runs/41833432977) at final tested SHA. D/E real-browser test proves preview on active and archived project, then actual rename/archive/restore, Quality v0 preservation and backend restart. No owner/native test.

**Important limits:** Review is not a complete filesystem manifest and does not authorize permanent delete. A concurrent experiment or investigation can change dependencies without changing the project lifecycle revision, so actual deletion must freeze/recheck writes and use an owner-approved, crash-recoverable journal and quarantine design. Full safety proposal at [P1 deletion impact and design](./P1_PROJECT_DELETION_IMPACT_AND_SAFETY_DESIGN_2026-11-04.md). P1 native gates and closure remain OPEN; permanent deletion NOT IMPLEMENTED.

## Latest verified increment: cross-window archive protection (4 November 2026)
**Source/test SHA:** [`6556f3ef4fde`](https://github.com/Sonkhe-Shongwe/PilotODIN/commit/6556f3ef4fde630bbc3476572b203ae343e19654). The Project Manager publishes only a bounded project-ID/revision/archived signal to localStorage after a successful rename/archive/restore. Other browser windows treat this as an **untrusted hint**, re-check `GET /api/projects/manage` against the authoritative revision before reflecting the status/name, and refresh their active catalogue. A project archived in another tab is explicitly shown as archived/restore-required without silently clearing this window's selected project or remounting its workspace. A foreign tab's stale shared selection no longer invalidates a separately mounted workspace just because the catalogue omits the selection. Restoring through another tab clears the archived warning and selector placeholder.

**Automated verification, all green at the exact SHA:** [isolated frontend #41838999119](https://github.com/Sonkhe-Shongwe/PilotODIN/actions/runs/41838999119) **145/145 Node, TypeScript/Vite build, 5/5 isolated Chromium**. [real-Uvicorn/Chromium #41838999113](https://github.com/Sonkhe-Shongwe/PilotODIN/actions/runs/41838999113) **four scenarios PASS**: original A/B import/recovery 1/1, C Quality consent/restart 1/1 and D/E Project Manager tests **2/2**. The new two-page synthetic test performs a real backend archive/restore of project D, sends a same-origin second-tab versioned storage hint, refuses an unverified forged future revision, and confirms the first tab retains its exact mounted DOM pane, project selector identity and original Quality history. No unreviewed writes to user data.

**Scope limits:** This test proves a hosted Chromium two-tab notification/recovery path, **not** native Windows WebView2 shared storage between actual desktop windows, not physical scaling, not persistence of every unsaved input value after an OS-level crash, and not the full multi-window concurrency/running-task freeze needed for irreversible deletion. Automatic cross-window notification requires shared localStorage; backend 404 remains the authority for archived project operations. **No permanent DELETE capability**, data purge or installed-app mutation. Initial real-HTTP [run #41838635839](https://github.com/Sonkhe-Shongwe/PilotODIN/actions/runs/41838635839) failed due to a test-only browser-context variable reference after reaching the archive checks. Fixed explicitly in [`6556f3ef4fde`](https://github.com/Sonkhe-Shongwe/PilotODIN/commit/6556f3ef4fde630bbc3476572b203ae343e19654); final hosted run green. P1 native owner acceptance and phase closure remain OPEN.

## Latest verified increment: archived-project HTTP mutation guard (4 November 2026)
**Tested SHA:** [`1ac2587920cd`](https://github.com/Sonkhe-Shongwe/PilotODIN/commit/1ac2587920cd0e482102ce96a24c0b739ad220fe). **P1-M4 request-entry protection**: a project archived in another window is refused for state-changing HTTP routes even if an old tab still has its project loaded. The local authenticated `desktop_session_guard` checks the authoritative `project_lifecycle` state on POST, PUT, PATCH and DELETE calls before entering the route, including direct `/api/projects/{id}/...` and indirect `/api/experiments/{id}/{select|predict|cancel}`, `/api/investigations/{id}/{runs|autonomy/...}` and `/api/reports/{id}/revisions`. The actual project is resolved by fixed, parameterized SQL joins, not a client-supplied project hint. For archived projects, the response is HTTP 409, with a clear restore instruction; no downstream mutation executes. `GET` remains unchanged; project import and explicit lifecycle rename/restore still function. In desktop-session mode, a missing/incorrect secret fails HTTP 401 **before** archive status is inspected.

**Verified exact scope:** [backend lifecycle CI #41840614063](https://github.com/Sonkhe-Shongwe/PilotODIN/actions/runs/41840614063), **14/14 disposable pytest PASS** (backend tested on source ancestor `3dd784f47153`, production backend unchanged afterward); [frontend #41840976435](https://github.com/Sonkhe-Shongwe/PilotODIN/actions/runs/41840976435) **145/145 Node, TS/Vite build, 5/5 Chromium PASS**; [real HTTP #41840976488](https://github.com/Sonkhe-Shongwe/PilotODIN/actions/runs/41840976488) **4/4 independent Uvicorn+Chromium scenarios PASS**, including the synthetic two-tab archive followed by actual 409 denials on Quality commit, investigation creation, target context and report creation, then Restore. Original user datasets and Windows installed app untouched.

**Important limitation, not a waiver:** This is a **request-entry check**, not an end-to-end SQLite transaction freeze. An already-started write could theoretically race a concurrent archive after the preflight. It does not cover direct Python function calls/background worker internals or every non-HTTP write. Prior to any irreversible Delete, require centralized commit-time write checks, authoritative ownership inventory, durable exclusion/journal/quarantine and crash recovery. P1 native owner gates, real multi-Window WebView2, full resource, scaling and Narrator checks remain **OPEN**. No DELETE endpoint is added.

## Latest verified increment: SQLite transaction-bound Quality archive fence (4 November 2026)
**Exact latest tested source:** [`8a4c66a262fd`](https://github.com/Sonkhe-Shongwe/PilotODIN/commit/8a4c66a262fd7525c45180b007989473aecaa110). **P1-M5 HOSTED PASS / NATIVE OPEN.**

- **Implemented:** Under Quality's own `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` SQLite write transactions, `quality._require_active_for_write(con,pid)` now rechecks the same database's authoritative `project_lifecycle.archived_at` before modifying Quality data. This covers both `quality_approvals.prepare` (new version-bound approval receipt) and `quality_approvals.commit` (approved correction, undo and restore), plus direct legacy `quality.autopilot`, `quality.decide`, `quality.approve_imputation`, `quality.undo` and `quality.restore` internal paths. A stale project object loaded before an archive cannot authorize Quality writes afterward; rejection is HTTP-compatible QualityError **409** and leaves immutable originals/history/receipts untouched. The previous HTTP archived-write preflight remains in place.
- **Archive-first and write-first proof:** Real SQLite threads were coordinated using an explicit test-only synchronization event inside the Quality write transaction. When the Quality transaction holds SQLite's write lock, `project_lifecycle.change(...,'archive',revision)` must wait and then commit only after the Quality decision succeeds. When archiving has already committed, synthetic calls carrying a cached pre-archive project object to seven Quality mutation paths fail 409 without editing receipt/version/decision state. The archive-first route does not depend on the HTTP preflight.
- **Verified:** [lifecycle backend #41852781940](https://github.com/Sonkhe-Shongwe/PilotODIN/actions/runs/41852781940) **16/16 pytest PASS**, source ancestor `5a85d5e03b63` (all production code identical to latest); [real HTTP #41853026518](https://github.com/Sonkhe-Shongwe/PilotODIN/actions/runs/41853026518) **4/4 Uvicorn + Chromium integration scenarios PASS** on exact final tested SHA, including previously accepted A/B import/recovery, C Quality consent/commit/undo/restore/restart and D/E project library/two-page archived mutation rejection. Frontend source unchanged since [145/145 Node, TypeScript/Vite build, 5/5 isolated Chromium #41840976435](https://github.com/Sonkhe-Shongwe/PilotODIN/actions/runs/41840976435); that earlier run remains historical frontend-only evidence, **not** a fresh frontend run at `8a4c66`.
- **Limitations:** No new DELETE endpoint, file removal, data import or native Windows interaction. This protects the specified **Quality write transactions**, not all report, experiment, investigation, model-worker or saved-analysis writes; read-only Quality paths may lazily initialize state, not evaluated as a project-wide write fence. An irreversible Delete still requires complete cross-module commit-time protection, durable journal/quarantine and recovery. Physical Windows scaling/Narrator/DEV native owner acceptance remain OPEN. P1 **OPEN**.

## Latest verified increment: report, saved Explore and investigation archive transaction fence (4 November 2026)
**Exact latest integration-tested source:** [`0920202f5089`](https://github.com/Sonkhe-Shongwe/PilotODIN/commit/0920202f5089a9002427f2e6bd23a41d52d0fae9). **P1-M6 HOSTED PASS / NATIVE OPEN.**

- **Implemented:** Reusable `project_lifecycle.require_active_for_write(con, project_id)` checks project ownership and archival state from SQLite inside the caller's existing `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` write transaction. Added to `reports.capture_quality` and `reports.save`, `saved_analyses.save`, `investigations.create` and `investigations.run` before their managed records are inserted or updated. For an archived project, the domain returns its normal HTTP-compatible 409 with an explicit Restore instruction, even if computation began with a cached pre-archive project. `saved_analyses.save` now initializes its schema *before* acquiring the transaction, as its `executescript` can otherwise auto-commit; inside the transaction it also re-checks the working dataset version to prevent a stale analytical snapshot from being saved. `investigations.create` similarly rechecks Quality version inside the locked transaction. No irreversible operation, source file modification or new UI.
- **Verification:** [lifecycle backend #41854793319](https://github.com/Sonkhe-Shongwe/PilotODIN/actions/runs/41854793319) **18/18 disposable pytest PASS** at source ancestor `ecacb0b207a6` with all production code identical to the exact integration-tested SHA; [real-Uvicorn/Chromium #41855153909](https://github.com/Sonkhe-Shongwe/PilotODIN/actions/runs/41855153909) **4/4 independent browser scenarios PASS** on `0920202f5089`, retaining Quality approval/undo/restore/restart and two-tab project manager acceptance. New backend tests prove archive-first denial of five report/Explore/investigation operations given stale cached project metadata; unchanged original bytes and linked record counts; successful restored writes; and a synchronized report-write-first / archive-second concurrency ordering under SQLite. [Isolated backend source recovery #41854469391](https://github.com/Sonkhe-Shongwe/PilotODIN/actions/runs/41854469391) passed on the earlier production-code commit, and unchanged frontend retains prior [145/145 Node + build + 5/5 hosted Chromium](https://github.com/Sonkhe-Shongwe/PilotODIN/actions/runs/41840976435) evidence only (not rerun at the newest SHA).
- **Limits:** This transaction check applies only to **listed SQL paths**. It does not provide a universal project lock across models, ODIN background tasks, target business context, other auxiliary writes or filesystem side effects. A write committing before archive might experience a post-commit read/reload race; no crash recovery or exhaustive file ownership guarantee is claimed. Permanent Delete is **NOT IMPLEMENTED**, P1 remains **OPEN**, and native owner Windows WebView2, Narrator and physical scaling/Snap/resource acceptance remain unverified.
